What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I’ve kind of stopped doing the things that usually make me feel like myself. I’m not really starting anything anymore, even things I normally care about.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the activities you used to like, even if you're not starting them as often?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Even when I think about doing them, like going out for photos or helping with the kids, it just feels kind of flat. I don’t get that same pull to start, and even my calendar stuff—which I used to like keeping in order—feels heavy now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad most of the time. It’s more like I feel kind of flat and low, but not necessarily crying or anything like that. I’ve noticed it, but I’m not sure how to label it.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in things that you normally would, like eating your favorite foods or spending time with loved ones?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. The main thing is the stuff that usually feels like me, like taking photography walks or mentoring the kids, just doesn’t feel rewarding lately. Even keeping my calendar clean, which I normally like, feels heavy. I just don’t really start those things anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed any major change. I’ll sleep maybe two or three hours, wake up before dawn, and then I feel like I should just start answering emails or updating appointment lists instead of feeling tired.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m moving slower.
